# OpenSubtitles v2024

This is a new collection of translated movie and TV subtitles from [opensubtitles.org](http://www.opensubtitles.org/), released by the [OPUS project](https://opus.nlpl.eu/legacy/OpenSubtitles-v2024.php).

**IMPORTANT:** If you use the OpenSubtitle corpus: Please, add a link to [http://www.opensubtitles.org/](http://www.opensubtitles.org/) to your website and to your reports and publications produced with the data!

This is a slightly cleaner version of the subtitle collection using improved sentence alignment and better language checking.

## Dataset Details

- **Release:** 2024 (OPUS stats: 93 languages, 3,377 bitexts, 41.03G tokens, 5.33G sentence fragments)
- **This repository:** all 2,997 available moses pairs, in both directions (5,994 configs), ~35.2B rows (~17.6B unique sentence pairs), ~1.94 TB parquet

## Structure

Each config (e.g. `en-es`) contains a single `train` split with aligned sentence pairs:

```python
{
    "id": "0",
    "translation": {
        "en": "English sentence",
        "es": "Spanish sentence"
    }
}
```

The format is the same as `alamios/OpenSubtitles-v2018`, **except** for the `meta` field
(year, imdbId, subtitleId, sentenceIds), which is not available in the OPUS v2024 release.


Each pair is available in **both directions** for convenience:
- `en-es`: English as first language, Spanish as second
- `es-en`: Spanish as first language, English as second

## Usage

```python
from datasets import load_dataset

# Load a specific pair
ds = load_dataset("alamios/OpenSubtitles-v2024", "en-es", split="train")

# Or in streaming mode
ds = load_dataset("alamios/OpenSubtitles-v2024", "en-es", split="train", streaming=True)

for example in ds.take(5):
    print(example["translation"]["en"])
    print(example["translation"]["es"])
```

## Available Pairs

All 2997 language pairs of the OPUS v2024 release, in both directions (5994 configs).
Each config `XX-YY` contains the `XX`→`YY` sentences; `YY-XX` is the reverse direction.
The 93 languages are:

af, am, an, ar, as, ast, az, be, bg, bn, br, bs, ca, cs, cy, da, de, el, en, en_ze, eo, es, es_419, et, eu, fa, fi, fr, ga, gd, gl, he, hi, hr, hu, hy, id, ig, is, it, ja, ka, kk, km, kn, ko, ku, lb, lt, lv, mk, ml, mn, mr, ms, my, ne, nl, nn, no, oc, or, pl, pt, ro, ru, sd, se, si, sk, sl, so, sq, sr, sv, sw, ta, te, th, tl, tr, tt, uk, ur, vi, yue, zh

## Differences from v2018

| Feature | v2018 | v2024 |
|---------|-------|-------|
| Languages | 62 | 93 |
| Tokens | 22.10G | 41.03G |
| Sentence fragments | 3.35G | 5.33G |
| Metadata (year, imdbId) | Yes | No |
| Format | Moses (3 files) | Moses (2 files) |

## Citation

If you use this dataset, please cite:

```
P. Lison and J. Tiedemann, 2016, OpenSubtitles2016: Extracting Large Parallel Corpora from Movie and TV Subtitles. In Proceedings of the 10th International Conference on Language Resources and Evaluation (LREC 2016)
```

And the OPUS project:

```
J. Tiedemann, 2012, Parallel Data, Tools and Interfaces in OPUS. In Proceedings of the 8th International Conference on Language Resources and Evaluation (LREC 2012)
```

Unknown. The OPUS project states: "We do not own any of the text from which the data has been extracted. We only offer files that we believe we are free to redistribute."
